<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We consider the continuum spin field constructed from the nearest-neighbor two-dimensional $`O(3)`$ model by the fixed prescription of the companion paper. We prove that its vector two-point spectral measure has a positive atom corresponding to the lowest mass, separated by a positive gap from all remaining mass support.

- **claim-isolated-lowest-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): For the field $\phi$ just specified, there exist $m_1,Z,\delta>0$ and a positive measure $\rho_{\mathrm{rest}}$ such that $$\rho=Z\delta_{m_1^2}+\rho_{\mathrm{rest}}, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\rho_{\mathrm{rest}}\subset[(m_1+\delta)^2,\infty). \label{intro:atom}$$ In particular, $m_1$ is the smallest mass in the support of the spin-field two-point function.
  - Explanation: The vector two-point spectral measure of the continuum field has an isolated positive atom at the lowest mass, and a strictly positive gap separates it from the rest of the mass support.
- **claim-construction-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-contracts)): There are integers $K_0,M_0>0$ and constants $c,C>0$ with the following properties. Write $w_k=M_0 2^k$, and let $Z_N(t,w)$ be the partition function on a torus of time length $t$ and spatial circumference $w$.* 1. *In the cutoff plane, all joint moments of the variables <a href="#inp:field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:field">[inp:field]</a> converge to those of the Euclidean field $\phi$. The limiting Schwinger distributions are tempered, Euclidean invariant, and $O(3)$ covariant. Their Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction is the Wightman field of Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a>, with unique vacuum $\Omega$ and $$H_{\mathrm{phys}}\|_{\Omega^\perp}\ge m>0.$$ For some real test $f$ supported strictly in positive time, the one-field reflection norm $\mathbb E[\phi^1(\theta f)\phi^1(f)]$ is strictly positive.* 2. *For all $N\ge K_0$ and $k\ge0$, $$0\le \Delta_N(w_k) :=4\log Z_N(w_k,w_k)-\log Z_N(2w_k,2w_k) \le C e^{-c w_k}. \label{inp:defect}$$* 3. *On these square tori and on the cutoff plane, the component moment measures $$S_{r,N}^{\boldsymbol i} =(B_Na^2)^r\sum_{x_1,\ldots,x_r} \mathbb E\!\left[\prod_{j=1}^r q_{x_j}^{i_j}\right] \delta_{(x_1,\ldots,x_r)}$$ are nonnegative. For arbitrary translated unit boxes $Q_1,\ldots,Q_r$, $$S_{r,N}^{\boldsymbol i}(Q_1\times\cdots\times Q_r) \le C^r r!, \label{inp:majorant}$$ uniformly in $N\ge K_0$, the square torus, the box positions, and the component indices.
  - Explanation: The continuum construction supplies Euclidean Schwinger distributions with a spectral gap above the vacuum, exponentially small doubling defects of square-torus partition functions, and factorial moment bounds on spin sums.
- **claim-positive-mass-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-kernel)): One has $0<m_*<\infty$. At separated Euclidean points, the like-component two-point function is the continuous kernel $$C(t,b)=\int\rho(d\mu^2)\int_{\mathbb R}\frac{dp}{2\pi} \frac{e^{ipb-tE(p,\mu)}}{2E(p,\mu)}, \qquad E(p,\mu)=\sqrt{p^2+\mu^2},\quad t>0. \label{inp:euclidean}$$ It is strictly positive at every nonzero Euclidean separation, and there is a constant $C_0$ such that $$0<C(t,b)\le C_0e^{-m_*\sqrt{t^2+b^2}} \quad\text{when }\sqrt{t^2+b^2}\ge1. \label{inp:decay}$$
  - Explanation: The bottom of the mass support is positive and finite, and the like-component Euclidean two-point kernel is continuous, strictly positive off coincidence and exponentially decaying.
- **claim-moment-tail-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-inp-tails)): Fix finitely many real component test shapes of bounded support. Their arbitrary translates, in the cutoff plane or in the square tori of Proposition <a href="#inp:contracts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:contracts">2</a>, satisfy $$\mathbb E\|X_f^i\|^r\le A^r r!,\qquad \mathbb Ee^{\kappa\|X_f^i\|}\le C \label{inp:tails-bound}$$ for some $A,C,\kappa>0$ independent of the cutoff, volume, and translations. The constants can also be chosen uniformly for test families of bounded supremum and support diameter. For each fixed $r$, tests supported in a common bounded set obey $$\\|X_f^i-X_g^i\\|_{L^r}\le C_r\\|f-g\\|_\infty, \label{inp:test-difference}$$ with the same uniformity under translations of that set.
  - Explanation: Smeared component spin sums obey factorial moment and exponential tail bounds that are uniform in cutoff, volume and translations, together with a Lipschitz-type difference bound for tests.
- **claim-pressure-trace-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-tra-pressure)): For each $N\ge K_0$, define $P_N(t,w)=(tw)^{-1}\log Z_N(t,w)$ and $P_{\infty,N}=\inf_{t,w}P_N(t,w)$. There are constants $c,C>0$, independent of $N$, such that, whenever $\min(t,w)$ is sufficiently large, $$0\le P_N(t,w)-P_{\infty,N} \le Ce^{-c\min(t,w)}. \label{tra:pressure-bound}$$ If $g_N(w)=a^{-1}\log\lambda_{0,N}(w)-wP_{\infty,N}$, then $$0\le g_N(w)\le Cwe^{-cw},\qquad 0\le\log R_{N,w}(t)\le Ctwe^{-c\min(t,w)}. \label{tra:trace}$$ The bound for $g_N$ holds at every sufficiently large allowed circumference.
  - Explanation: Rectangular torus pressures converge exponentially to their infimum, which gives exponential bounds on the ground-state energy shift and on the logarithm of the transfer heat trace.
- **claim-uniform-transfer-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-tra-gap)): There are $c_0>0$ and $w_0<\infty$ such that $$e_N(w)\ge c_0\qquad(N\ge K_0,\ w\ge w_0,\ w\in a_N\mathbb N).$$
  - Explanation: The lowest positive transfer energy is bounded below by a positive constant, uniformly in the cutoff and in all sufficiently large allowed circumferences.
- **claim-slab-insertion** (bound, [source](#stmt-tra-insertion)): One has $\\|A_F\\|\le\\|F\\|_\infty$. Write $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{cyl},N,w}F=\langle\Omega_{N,w},A_F\Omega_{N,w}\rangle$ for the infinite-time cylinder expectation. If $t>r$, then $$\left\|\mathbb E_{\mathrm{tor},N;t,w}F -\mathbb E_{\mathrm{cyl},N,w}F\right\| \le\\|F\\|_\infty \bigl(s_{N,w}(t-r)+s_{N,w}(t)\bigr). \label{tra:torus-cylinder}$$
  - Explanation: A bounded observable inserted in a time slab gives an operator whose norm is at most the supremum norm, and torus expectations approach infinite-cylinder expectations up to excited-state trace terms.
- **claim-torus-plane-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-tra-comparison)): Let $w=w_k$ be sufficiently large. If the support of a bounded observable $F$ has a lift contained in a rectangle of extent at most $3w/4$ in each coordinate, then $$\left\|\mathbb E_{\mathrm{tor},N;w,w}F-\mathbb E_{\mathrm{plane},N}F\right\| \le C\\|F\\|_\infty e^{-cw}, \label{tra:plane-comparison}$$ uniformly in $N\ge K_0$. The assertion holds for bounded measurable observables. Comparison of this square with its infinite-time cylinder requires only the time extent bound.
  - Explanation: For observables supported within three quarters of the circumference in each direction, square-torus and cutoff-plane expectations differ by an exponentially small amount, uniformly in the cutoff.
- **claim-field-moment-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-tra-moments)): Fix a number of component tests, chosen from families with uniformly bounded suprema and support diameters. If their translated supports together satisfy the extent condition of Proposition <a href="#tra:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tra:comparison">8</a>, then the square-torus and cutoff-plane expectations of their product differ by at most $Ce^{-cw}$. The constants may depend on the number and shapes of the tests but not on their separate translations, the cutoff, or $w$.
  - Explanation: Products of smeared component tests supported within the allowed extent have exponentially close torus and plane expectations, with constants independent of the separate translations.
- **claim-quadratic-covariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-par-covariance)): Let $P$ and $Q$ be monomials in spin components for the law <a href="#par:graph-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="par:graph-law">[par:graph-law]</a>, of even total degrees $r$ and $s$, with nonempty site lists $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$. Put $$G=\max_{x\in\mathcal A,\ y\in\mathcal B}\mathbb E(q_x^3q_y^3).$$ There is a constant $C_{r,s}$, depending only on the two degrees, such that $$\label{par:quadratic-bound} \|\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}(P,Q)\|\le C_{r,s}G^2.$$ If either monomial is constant, its covariance is zero.
  - Explanation: On any finite rotator graph, the covariance of two even spin monomials is bounded by a constant times the square of the largest two-point correlation between their site lists.
- **claim-angle-association-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-par-association)): The marginal law of $\alpha$ in <a href="#par:angles" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="par:angles">[par:angles]</a> is positively associated. For even-degree monomials each using a single component, covariances are nonnegative when the components agree and nonpositive when they differ. The same covariance signs hold for the two-component plane rotator.
  - Explanation: The angle marginal is positively associated, which fixes the signs of covariances of single-component even monomials for like and unlike components.
- **claim-even-sector-odd-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-par-even-gap)): On the even subspace perpendicular to the vacuum, $$\label{par:even-norm} \bigl\\|T\big\|_{\mathrm{even}\cap\Omega^\perp}\bigr\\| \le\tau^2.$$ There is a real unit $\tau$-eigenfunction $\psi$ that is odd under simultaneous spin inversion and, after a component permutation, odd under the reflection of component $3$ alone. It satisfies $$\label{par:overlap} \langle \Omega,\|\psi\|\rangle^2\ge\frac{\tau}{1+\tau}.$$
  - Explanation: On the even vacuum complement the transfer operator has norm at most the square of the lowest eigenvalue, so the lowest excitation is odd and has a quantitative overlap with the vacuum after taking absolute values.
- **claim-transfer-energy-visibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-vis-proposition)): Along the prescribed circumferences $w_k=M_0 2^k$, $$\label{vis:gap} \liminf_{k\to\infty}\;\liminf_{N\to\infty} e_N(w_k)\ge m_*.$$
  - Explanation: Along the dyadic circumferences, the double limit inferior of the lowest transfer energy is at least the bottom of the two-point mass support.
- **claim-row-connection-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-vis-row-lemma)): The probability that an open cluster meets both the row at time $0$ and the row at time $d$ satisfies $$\label{vis:row-connection} \mathbb P_{\mathrm{tor}}(0\longleftrightarrow d) \ge c\,e^{-(d+a)e_N(w)}.$$
  - Explanation: The probability that an open sign cluster joins the two rows is bounded below by an exponential in the lowest transfer energy.
- **claim-positive-cluster-weight** (bound, [source](#stmt-vis-weight-lemma)): There exist $\epsilon,c_0>0$ and fixed thresholds $N_0,w_0$ such that for $N\ge N_0$, $w=w_k\ge w_0$, and every center $s$, $$\label{vis:positive-weight} \mathbb P_{\mathrm{tor}}\{S(h_s,h'_s)\ge\epsilon\}\ge c_0.$$
  - Explanation: With probability bounded below uniformly in the cutoff and the center, the normalized cluster weight of the two translated tests exceeds a fixed positive threshold.
- **claim-linear-state-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-cou-linear)): There is a constant $C$ such that, for every sufficiently large dyadic circumference $w=w_k$, $$\label{cou:linear-eq} \limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathcal N_{N,w}(u_0)\le Cw, \qquad u_0=\frac32m_*.$$
  - Explanation: The number of transfer energies below three halves of the mass bottom grows at most linearly in the dyadic circumference in the cutoff limit.
- **claim-binomial-cutoff-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-cou-polynomial)): For all sufficiently large $w$, uniformly in $y\in[u_0,u_1]$, $$\begin{aligned} Q_{w,y}(e^{-E})&\ge1-Ce^{-cD^{3/4}} &&(0\le E\le y),\label{cou:poly-low}\\ Q_{w,y}(e^{-E})&\le Ce^{-cD^{3/4}}e^{-t_0E} &&(E\ge y+b_w).\label{cou:poly-high} \end{aligned}$$ Every nonzero monomial of $Q_{w,y}$ has degree between $t_0$ and $D$, and the sum of the absolute values of its coefficients is at most $3^D$.
  - Explanation: A binomial upper-tail polynomial approximates an energy cutoff: it is close to one below the cutoff, exponentially small and summable above it, with controlled degrees and coefficient sum.
- **claim-spectral-moment-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-moments)): For every integer $n\ge0$ and every compact interval $I$ with nonempty interior, $$\label{mass:moments-eq} \lim_{\substack{w\to\infty\\w=w_k}}\limsup_{N\to\infty} \max_{p\in I} \left\| \int x^n\,\nu_{N,w,p}(dx) -\int_\mathbb Re^{-ipb}C_f(n,b)\,db \right\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: Moments of the finite-circumference measures at fixed momentum converge uniformly over compact momentum intervals to the Fourier transform of the continuum smeared correlation.
- **claim-finite-low-mass-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-finite-support)): The set $$\{\mu\in[m_*,5m_*/4]:\mu^2\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\rho\}$$ is finite.
  - Explanation: Only finitely many masses of the support lie in the window from the mass bottom up to five quarters of it.

        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ and $Q$ be monomials in spin components for the law <a href=\"#par:graph-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"par:graph-law\">[par:graph-law]</a>, of even total degrees $r$ and $s$, with nonempty site lists $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **normalized area measure on the sphere** ([source](#inp:field)): Let $\sigma$ be normalized area measure on $S^2$.
- **bottom of the mass support** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Write $m_*$ for the bottom of the mass support of $\rho$; the source gap and nonzero one-field norm imply $0<m_*<\infty$.
- **site list** ([source](#par:graph-law)): A list of sites records one entry per factor of a monomial; repeated sites remain separate entries.
- **positively associated law** ([source](#par:angles)): A law is *positively associated* if the covariance of every pair of bounded increasing functions is nonnegative.
- **crossing pair** ([source](#par:pair-coefficient)): Call a pair crossing if it joins the two lists.
- **even and odd row function** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A row function is *even* or *odd* according to its parity under simultaneous inversion of every row spin.
- **sign cluster** ([source](#pm-h-18)): We call the connected components *sign clusters*.
- **square box notation** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Write $Q_r(s)=s+[-r,r]^2$.
- **barrier** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Call such a set a *barrier*.
- **short-way and long-way crossing** ([source](#pm-h-20)): A *short-way* crossing joins the sides at distance $2$; a *long-way* crossing joins those at distance $12$.
- **translated test pair** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Fix a nonzero nonnegative $h\in C_c^\infty((-1,1)^2)$, and let $h_s$ be its translate to a lattice center $s$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-doubling-defect | doubling defect of the square partition function | ≤ | $C e^{-c w_k}$ | uniform in the cutoff and in the dyadic index |
| q-moment-majorant | component moment measure of unit boxes | ≤ | $C^r r!$ | uniform in box positions and component indices |
| q-hamiltonian-gap | physical Hamiltonian on the vacuum complement | ≥ | $m$ | continuum theory |
| q-pressure-convergence | pressure minus its infimum | ≤ | $Ce^{-c\min(t,w)}$ | whenever the smaller side is sufficiently large |
| q-ground-energy-shift | ground-state energy shift | ≤ | $Cwe^{-cw}$ | sufficiently large allowed circumference |
| q-heat-trace-log | logarithm of the normalized transfer trace | ≤ | $Ctwe^{-c\min(t,w)}$ | uniform in the cutoff |
| q-transfer-gap-floor | lowest positive transfer energy | ≥ | $c_0$ | uniform in the cutoff |
| q-plane-comparison-error | torus-to-plane expectation difference | ≤ | $C\\|F\\|_\infty e^{-cw}$ | bounded observables with support within the three-quarter extent |
| q-covariance-bound | covariance of even spin monomials | ≤ | $C_{r,s}G^2$ | constant depends only on the two degrees |
| q-even-sector-norm | transfer norm on the even vacuum complement | ≤ | $\tau^2$ | tau is the exponential of minus the lattice spacing times the lowest transfer energy |
| q-odd-overlap | squared overlap of the vacuum with the modulus of the odd eigenfunction | ≥ | $\frac{\tau}{1+\tau}$ | explicit in tau |
| q-visibility-limit | double limit inferior of the lowest transfer energy | ≥ | $m_*$ | cutoff limit first, then circumference to infinity |
| q-row-connection | probability of a cluster meeting both rows | ≥ | $c\,e^{-(d+a)e_N(w)}$ | row separation d on the order of the square root of the circumference |
| q-cluster-weight-probability | probability that the normalized cluster weight exceeds the threshold | ≥ | $c_0$ | uniform in the cutoff and the center |
| q-visibility-final-bound | limit superior of the exponentiated transfer energy | ≤ | $Cw^2\bigl(e^{-m_*d}+e^{-cw}\bigr)$ | dyadic circumference, row separation fixed by the square root of the circumference |
| q-linear-count | number of transfer energies below the counting threshold | ≤ | $Cw$ | threshold three halves of the mass bottom |
| q-counting-threshold | counting energy threshold | = | $\frac32m_*$ | fixed multiple of the mass bottom |
| q-two-excitation-threshold | twice the lowest transfer energy | ≥ | $\frac74m_*$ | consequence of the visibility estimate |
| q-energy-shift | energy shift of the cutoff polynomial | = | $D^{-1/8}$ | depends on the circumference through D |
| q-intermediate-scale | intermediate time scale | = | $\lfloor w^{1/2}\rfloor$ | function of the circumference |
| q-poly-lower | cutoff polynomial below the cutoff energy | ≥ | $1-Ce^{-cD^{3/4}}$ | energies up to y |
| q-poly-upper | cutoff polynomial above the shifted cutoff energy | ≤ | $Ce^{-cD^{3/4}}e^{-t_0E}$ | energies at least y plus the shift |
| q-poly-coefficient-sum | sum of absolute values of polynomial coefficients | ≤ | $3^D$ | degree at most D |
| q-doubling-recursion | doubling factor of the state count | ≤ | $2\bigl(1+Ce^{-cD^{3/4}}\bigr)$ | thresholds in the stated energy window |
| q-masses-versus-states | states required by distinct masses near the bottom | ≤ | $\mathcal N_{N,w}(u_0)$ | J distinct masses, number of momenta proportional to the circumference |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-inputs** [claim](#stmt-inp-contracts) / [proof](#stmt-inp-contracts): Quote the quantitative inputs of the continuum construction: reconstruction with a gap, exponentially small square doubling defects, factorial moment majorants. -> Contract used by all later estimates
- **s-kernel** [claim](#stmt-inp-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inp-kernel): Show mass support lies above the gap, then use the Källén-Lehmann representation and Euclidean invariance to get a positive decaying kernel. -> Positive finite bottom of mass support and kernel decay
- **s-tails** [claim](#stmt-inp-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inp-tails): Cover tests by unit boxes, bound even moments by the majorant, sum the exponential series. -> Uniform moment and tail bounds
- **s-pressure** [claim](#stmt-tra-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tra-pressure): Monotonicity of pressures plus telescoping of the dyadic doubling defects, then a trace identity. -> Pressure and trace bounds
- **s-gap** [claim](#stmt-tra-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tra-gap): Apply the trace bound at equal time and circumference and take logarithms. -> Uniform positive transfer gap
- **s-insertion** [claim](#stmt-tra-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tra-insertion): Pointwise kernel domination for the operator norm; subtract the ground-state term in the torus trace. -> Slab insertion and torus-cylinder bound
- **s-comparison** [claim](#stmt-tra-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tra-comparison): Compare successive tori with cylinders and sum the geometric errors over doubled widths. -> Torus-to-plane comparison
- **s-field-moments** [claim](#stmt-tra-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tra-moments): Truncate the variables, apply the comparison, and control the remainder by Hölder and tail bounds. -> Comparison of field moments
- **s-association** [claim](#stmt-par-association) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-association): Mixed partial derivatives of the log partition function give the lattice condition, hence association and covariance signs. -> Association of the angle marginal and covariance signs
- **s-covariance** [claim](#stmt-par-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-association-2): Pairing expansion, crossing-pair coefficient bound, and Fourier comparison to single-component monomials. -> Quadratic covariance bound
- **s-even-gap** [claim](#stmt-par-even-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-even-gap): Transfer the covariance bound to the cylinder, use density of even polynomials, then compare the odd eigenfunction with its modulus. -> Even-sector norm bound and odd overlap
- **s-row-lemma** [claim](#stmt-vis-row-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-vis-row-lemma): Expand the torus correlation of the sign detector in the eigenbasis and use cluster flipping off the connection event. -> Row connection lower bound
- **s-cluster-weight** [claim](#stmt-vis-weight-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-vis-weight-lemma): Lower bound the mean by the plane kernel and the comparison, bound the second moment, apply a second-moment inequality. -> Positive normalized cluster weight with positive probability
- **s-visibility** [claim](#stmt-vis-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-vis-weight-lemma-2): Barriers from planar crossing estimates and association, bridge paths merge weighted clusters, then take limits in the stated order. -> Lower bound of the lowest transfer energy by the mass bottom
- **s-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-cou-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cou-polynomial): Hoeffding tail bound for the binomial polynomial plus an extra damping factor for summability. -> Positive polynomial approximation to an energy cutoff
- **s-linear-count** [claim](#stmt-cou-linear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cou-polynomial-2): Exchange time and circumference, factor heat traces, derive the doubling recursion and iterate it along dyadic widths. -> Linear bound on the low-energy state count
- **s-spectral-moments** [claim](#stmt-mass-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-moments): Write the moments as Riemann sums of cylinder correlations, compare to the plane, and pass to the continuum integral. -> Moment convergence of momentum-resolved measures
- **s-finite-support** [claim](#stmt-mass-finite-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-finite-support): Distinct masses give disjoint continuous test functions requiring distinct low-energy states at each momentum; compare with the linear count. -> Finiteness of the low mass support
- **s-main-atom** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-finite-support-2): The minimum of the support is isolated, hence an atom of positive finite weight; choose the separation and split the measure. -> Isolated lowest-mass atom

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-isolated-lowest-mass-atom**: For the specified continuum spin field, the vector two-point spectral measure splits into a positive atom at the lowest mass plus a remainder whose support starts strictly above it by a positive gap.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-even-channel-pole**: Infer an isolated single-particle pole for operators that are even under simultaneous spin inversion. -> The atom is established only for the odd vector spin field; on the even sector all non-vacuum transfer excitations lie at twice the lowest energy or higher, so no single-particle conclusion follows.
- **anti-other-dimension**: Apply the isolated-mass conclusion to models in higher spacetime dimension. -> The proof uses a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice and a reconstructed spacetime with one space dimension; nothing is claimed elsewhere.
- **anti-external-field**: Use the result for lattice actions with an external field or altered bond structure. -> The lattice law has each bond once and no external field; the estimates were derived only for that law.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an analytic existence statement about a spectral measure of a continuum field obtained as a limit; the proof is a chain of limit and estimate arguments with no finite procedure whose output a program could compute and check, so no runnable reference implementation or exhaustive oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence statement about a spectral measure of a continuum field. It gives no specified procedure to turn into a library or module, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns a continuum quantum field limit, not a discrete simulation rule. It does not give a stepping scheme, an update rule or a physics engine primitive. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, solver or analysis utility is specified. The only quantities are existential constants, such as the isolated mass, its weight and the gap, which cannot be computed from the statement. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is a chain of analytic estimates and limits taken in a fixed order. It is not a finite procedure with inputs, outputs and a cost, and the constants are not effective. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation, index or container is introduced. The objects are operators, spectral measures and cluster configurations used only inside the proof. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no dataflow, kernel or parallel structure to map onto hardware. The bounds hold for the specified field at all cutoffs and say nothing about arithmetic precision or throughput. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary model or security notion appears. The spectral statement concerns a physical field and carries no cryptographic content. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss or generalisation claim. The positive association and spectral facts are proved for a specific lattice spin law and not for a data distribution. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective is optimised and no feasibility problem is posed. The infimum of the mass support and the infimum of the pressure are analytic quantities, not outputs of an optimisation method. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No nodes, messages, fault model or consensus property appear. The paper has no protocol to which the results could attach. |
| other (mathematical physics: spectral structure of the two-dimensional O(3) spin field) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural use is as a theoretical reference. Practitioners of lattice field theory may use it as a prior that the lowest mass in the spin two-point spectrum is an isolated atom. It supplies no construction, so no implementable contract is claimed. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-two-dimensional-setting** (hypothesis): The result is proved for a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice whose reconstructed spacetime has one time and one space dimension. Nothing is claimed in other dimensions. Blocked use: ['Applying the isolated lowest-mass conclusion to models in higher spacetime dimension', 'Quoting the atom-plus-gap structure for massless regimes']
- **limit-fixed-prescription** (hypothesis): The field is the one fixed by the companion prescription, with a dyadic spacing integer and a sufficiently large terminal coupling. The result holds only along that trajectory. Blocked use: ['Applying the atom result to fixed coarse lattices off the prescribed trajectory', 'Choosing the terminal coupling freely']
- **limit-zero-field-action** (hypothesis): The lattice law is the periodic nearest-neighbor law with every bond counted once and no external field. The estimates were derived only for that law. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We consider the continuum spin field constructed from the nearest-neighbor two-dimensional $`O(3)`$ model by the fixed prescription of the companion paper. We prove that its vector two-point spectral measure has a positive atom corresponding to the lowest mass, separated by a positive gap from all remaining mass support.
